where
\rho
is some permutation such that
\mathtt{dom}(\rho)\cap\{a,\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a),\ldots,\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{l}(a)\}=\emptyset
. Since each cycle of
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
mention at least one atom from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, it follows that there is some
1\leq k\leq l
such that
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{k}(a)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. As a consequence,
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{k}(a)\notin\mathtt{supp}(x)
. By Lemma
1
, it follows that
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(x)
, a contradiction.

Conversely, suppose that
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cap\mathtt{supp}(x)=\emptyset
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(x)=\emptyset
. By the definition of support, this is equivalent to say that
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x=x
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(x)=\emptyset
. In other words,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(x)=\emptyset\}\subseteq\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\mid\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x=x\}
. Therefore,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\mid\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x=x\}
is cofinite and so
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x=x
.

3.

Assume
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x=x
. By definition, this is equivalent to say that
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\mid\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x\neq x\}
is finite. Suppose, by contradiction, that
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\cdot x\neq x
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}^{\prime}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{n}^{\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(x)=\emptyset
. Take another list
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}^{\prime\prime}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{n}^{\prime\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}\cap(\mathtt{supp}(x)\cup\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}})=\emptyset
(there are a cofinite amount of them). Then
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}=\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
and so
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\cdot x\neq x
, proving that the set
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\mid\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x\neq x\}
is cofinite, a contradiction. The converse follows by the definition of

\mathsf{N}

.

Appendix 0.B
Proofs of Section
4.3
- Properties

See
4.1

Proof

1.

We want to prove that the conclusion of each of these rules implies its respective premise. But this follows directly from the fact that each rule corresponds to a unique class of terms. For example, if
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1}){{\approx}}_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{1},t_{0})
, then there is a proof
\Pi
of this judgment. However, the only possible rule applicable as a last step is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}})
, so we either have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t_{0}\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t_{1}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}
, or
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t_{0}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t_{1}\approx_{\tt\mathtt{C}}t_{0}
. The same reasoning applies to all the other rules.

2.

It’s sufficient to prove only the left-to-right case. The proof is by induction on the last rule applied.

•

The last rule is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{a})
. In this case,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash a\approx_{\tt C}a
. By
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{a})
, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\rho\cdot a\approx_{\tt C}\rho\cdot a
.

•

The last rule is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
. In this case,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)),
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
. Since
(\rho\circ\pi_{2})^{-1}\circ(\rho\circ\pi_{1})=\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}
, the result follows by an application of rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

•

The rules
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f}}),({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}}),({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{[a]})
and
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
follow by induction. Here we prove only the case of the rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
. In this case, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash[a]t\approx_{\tt C}[b]s
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
is taken such that it doesn’t occur in
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},a,b,t,s,\rho
. By induction, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash\rho\cdot((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t)\approx_{\tt C}\rho\cdot((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s)
, which is equivalent to
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(\rho(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\rho\cdot t)\approx_{\tt C}(\rho(b)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\rho\cdot s)
. Applying the
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
rule, we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash[\rho(a)]\rho\cdot t\approx_{\tt C}[\rho(b)]\rho\cdot s
, which is equivalent to
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\rho\cdot[a]t\approx_{\tt C}\rho\cdot[b]s
.

•

We will prove only the interesting cases.

–

Reflexivity.
The variable case is trivial because
\pi^{-1}\circ\pi=\mathtt{id}
and
\mathtt{id}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
always holds.

–

Symmetry.
Suppose
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we obtain
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
. Hence
\pi_{1}^{-1}\circ\pi_{2}=(\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1})^{-1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
. Therefore, the result follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

–

Transitivity.

Given nominal terms
s,t,u
and derivations
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash s\approx_{\tt C}u
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash u\approx_{\tt C}t
, we will show that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash s\approx_{\tt C}t
. We establish the result by induction on
s
, whose structure influences the other terms. We present only the suspension case here, as it is the most interesting. For
s\equiv\pi_{1}\cdot X
, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}u
. This forces
u\equiv\pi_{2}\cdot X
, which, in turn, forces
t\equiv\pi_{3}\cdot X
. Consequently,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{2}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{3}\cdot X
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)),
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
and
\pi_{3}^{-1}\circ\pi_{2}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})
. Since both
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
and
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})
are subsets of
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})
we have that
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}
and

\pi_{3}^{-1}\circ\pi_{2}
are in
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})
.
Therefore,
\pi_{3}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}=(\pi_{3}^{-1}\circ\pi_{2})\circ(\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1})
is also in
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})

and thus the result follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

3.

Direct consequence Equivariance and Equivalence.

4.

The proof is by induction on the structure of
s
. Here we show only the interesting cases.

•

s\equiv\pi_{1}\cdot Y
. This forces
t\equiv\pi_{2}\cdot Y
. Then we have
(\Upsilon\uplus\{\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot Y\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot Y
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), it follows that
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}((\Upsilon\uplus\{\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{Y})
.

–

Y\not\equiv X
.

Then
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}((\Upsilon\uplus\{\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{Y})=\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{Y})
and so the result follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

–

Y\equiv X
.

In this case, the condition
(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})\cap\mathtt{Atm}(s,t)=\emptyset
is the same as

(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})\cap(\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{1})\cup\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{2}))=\emptyset.

This implies
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1})\cap(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})=\emptyset
. Consequently, we have that
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
and the result follows by an application of rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

•

s\equiv[a]s^{\prime}
. We consider two cases for
t
:

–

Case 1:
t\equiv[a]t^{\prime}
.

In this situation, we have
(\Upsilon\uplus\{\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash[a]s^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t^{\prime}
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we obtain
(\Upsilon\uplus\{\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash s^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t^{\prime}
and since
(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})\cap\mathtt{Atm}(s,t)=\emptyset
implies
(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})\cap\mathtt{Atm}(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})=\emptyset
, the inductive hypothesis gives us
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash s^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t^{\prime}
, and the result follows by applying rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
.

–

Case 2:
t\equiv[b]t^{\prime}
.

Here, we have
(\Upsilon\uplus\{\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash[a]s^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[b]t^{\prime}
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we obtain
(\Upsilon\uplus\{\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},a,b,s^{\prime},t^{\prime},\pi)
. By the choice of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
, we have that
\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}=\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
and so
(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})\cap\mathtt{Atm}(s,t)=\emptyset
implies
(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cap\mathtt{Atm}(s,t)=\emptyset
. We claim that

(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cap\mathtt{Atm}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s^{\prime},(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime}))=\emptyset.

Indeed, suppose, by contradiction, that there is an atom, say
d
, such that
d\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
and
d\in\mathtt{Atm}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s^{\prime},(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime}))
. Then
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})(d)\in\mathtt{Atm}(s^{\prime})
. Note that
d
cannot be
a
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
. Thus
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})(d)=d
and hence
d\in\mathtt{Atm}(s^{\prime})
, contradicting
(\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cap\mathtt{Atm}(s,t)=\emptyset
.

Now, by induction, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime}
and the result follows by applying the rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
.

Appendix 0.C
Proofs of Section
4.3
- Soundness and Completeness

See
4.2

Since the proof of Completeness is much more elaborate, we will separate the proofs in different sections.

0.C.1
Soundness

Proof
(of Soundness)

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a model of
\mathtt{C}
. We must show for any valuation
\varsigma
that if
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
then
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is valid.

The proof proceeds by induction on the last rule applied in the derivation of the judgment. To illustrate, we show the cases where the last rule applied is…

•

({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
. In this case, we have

\rho^{-1}\circ\pi\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})

({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\rho\cdot X

Suppose
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is valid. Then:

\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\eta\cdot\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}\text{ for all $\eta\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in\Upsilon$.}

In particular,

\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\eta\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}\text{ for all $\eta\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon|_{X}$.}

By Pitts’ equivalence (Lemma
2
) it follows, for any
\eta\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in\Upsilon
, that for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\emptyset
,
(i)
\mathtt{dom}(\eta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\emptyset
,
and
(ii)
\eta_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
. From the condition
\rho^{-1}\circ\pi\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
and Lemma
1
, it follows that
(\rho^{-1}\circ\pi)\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
holds.

•

({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
. In this case, we have

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot u^{\prime}

({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash[a]t^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[b]u^{\prime}

Suppose
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is valid. We aim to show that
\llbracket[a]t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket[b]u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
, which is equivalent to:

\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(a,\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}).

From the validity of
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
, it follows that:

\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}.\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}\text{ for all }\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
is taken such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
we have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
for all
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon
. Furthermore, we can assume w.l.o.g. that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
is taken fresh for
\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
,
\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
and
\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
for all
X\in\mathtt{Var}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
. Then,

\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}\text{ holds for all }\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon.

Thus,
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is valid. By induction, we get

\llbracket(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}.

Claim
(1)

We claim that
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
and
b\notin\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
. We will prove only
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
, because the proof for
b\notin\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
follows by a similar argument. Suppose, by contradiction, that
a\in\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
. Under this assumption, we have
a\in\mathtt{supp}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
. Consequently, it follows that
a\in\mathtt{supp}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
. This implication leads to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\in\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
. However, this result contradicts the assumption that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
is fresh for
\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
.

Now, let’s use this information to proceed with the proof. Using that
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
, we infer
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}))
. Moreover, by definition, we have
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c}.(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c})\cdot\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
, which by Pitts’ equivalence means that
b\notin\mathtt{supp}(\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}))
. Therefore, we obtain
(a\ b)\cdot\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
. Hence, we can rewrite it as follows:

\displaystyle\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})

\displaystyle=(a\ b)\cdot\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(a,(a\ b)\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}).

To complete the proof, note that all we need to do is to show that
(a\ b)\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
. Then let’s prove this final step.

Claim
(2)

We claim that
(a\ b)\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
. On one hand, by Claim (1), we know that
b\notin\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
and since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
, this implies
(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket t\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
. On the other hand, we already know that
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
holds by induction. Thus,

\displaystyle(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow{}

\displaystyle(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow{}

\displaystyle((a\ b)\circ(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))\cdot\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=(a\ b)\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow{}

\displaystyle(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=(a\ b)\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow{}

\displaystyle\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=(a\ b)\cdot\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}.

This proves completes the proof that
\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(b,\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{A}}(a,\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
.

0.C.2
Completeness

The objective of this section is to show that the calculus is complete w.r.t. the nominal set semantics. The construction is long and complex, therefore, we will just present the roadmap of the constructions and results needed to prove Completeness, and only give the complete proof of the most challenging result (Lemma
7
). We refer the reader interested in the details to the extended version of this paper
[
9
]
.

Free Term Models.

The set of nominal terms
\mathbb{F}(\Sigma,\mathbb{V})
cannot form a model of commutative (
\mathtt{C}
) because it is not a nominal set under the usual permutation action. Specifically, the support of a suspension, such as
\rho\cdot X
, cannot be precisely determined, as
X
represents an unknown term. The standard approach is to work with the
free term models
, consisting of ground terms
\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
, over a signature
\Sigma
and a set of term-formers
\mathsf{D}
disjoint from
\Sigma
. Here we assume that
\Sigma
contains commutative function symbols, but
\mathsf{D}
doesn’t. The set of
free terms
is defined by the following grammar:

g,g^{\prime}::=a\mid[a]g\mid{\mathtt{f}}(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})\mid{\mathtt{d}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})

Here
\mathtt{f}:n
ranges over
\Sigma
and
\mathtt{d}:k
range over elements of
\mathsf{D}
.

As expected, to obtain nominal models for a theory
\mathtt{C}
we will work with the set of free terms quotient by provable equality modulo
\mathtt{C}
.
This quotient set, defined next, effectively incorporates both
\alpha
-equivalence and commutative behaviour, enabling us to construct a model that satisfies the desired properties in terms of nominal set semantics.

Definition 9
(Free terms up to
\mathtt{C}
)

Write
\overline{g}
for the set of free terms
g^{\prime}
such that
\emptyset\vdash g\approx_{\tt C}g^{\prime}
.
The
set of free terms
\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
up to
\mathtt{C}
, denoted as
\overline{\mathbb{F}}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
, is the set of equivalence classes (modulo
\mathtt{C}
) of free terms, i.e.,
\overline{\mathbb{F}}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})=\{\overline{g}\mid g\text{ is a free term}\}
.

In the following, we will abbreviate
\overline{\mathbb{F}}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
as
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathtt{C}}
. The next lemma presents a collection of diverse properties of free terms modulo
\mathtt{C}
. Notably, item
1
establishes that
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathtt{C}}
is a nominal set, while item
4
defines the support of the equivalence class of a free term modulo
\mathtt{C}
.

We will use the following elementary result from nominal set theory.

Lemma 3
(Pitts
[
2
]
)

If
\mathscr{X}
is a nominal set and
\sim
is an equivariant equivalence relation on
\mathscr{X}
and
x\in\mathscr{X}/_{\sim}
, then
\mathtt{supp}(x)=\bigcap\{\mathtt{supp}(g)\mid g\in x\}
.

Lemma 4
(Properties of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathtt{C}}
)

Let
g,g^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
. The following hold:

1.

The set
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathtt{C}}
with the action
\pi\cdot\overline{g}=\overline{\pi\cdot g}
is a nominal set and
\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})=\bigcap\{\mathtt{supp}(g^{\prime})\mid g^{\prime}\in\overline{g}\}
.

2.

The map
\mathtt{fn}(-):\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})\to\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fin}}(\mathbb{A})
is equivariant.

3.

If
g^{\prime}\in\overline{g}
then
\mathtt{fn}(g)=\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime})
.

4.

\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})=\mathtt{fn}(g)
.

Proof

1.

Direct consequence of Lemma
3
.

2.

For all
g\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
and all permutation
\pi
, we must prove that
\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g)=\pi\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g)
. This is done by induction on the ground term
g
. As an illustrative case, let
g\equiv[a]g^{\prime}
. By induction
\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g^{\prime})=\pi\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot[a]g^{\prime})=\mathtt{fn}([\pi(a)]\pi\cdot g^{\prime})=\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g^{\prime})\setminus\{\pi(a)\}

\displaystyle=\pi\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime})\setminus\pi\cdot\{a\}

\displaystyle=\pi\cdot(\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime})\setminus\{a\})

\displaystyle=\pi\cdot\mathtt{fn}([a]g^{\prime}).

3.

Suppose
g\sim g^{\prime}
. Then by definition there is a derivation
~\vdash g\approx_{\tt C}g^{\prime}
. The proof follows by induction on the last rule applied to obtain
~\vdash g\approx_{\tt C}g^{\prime}
. The only non-trivial case is when the last rule applied is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
. In this case,
g\equiv[a]g_{1}
and
g^{\prime}\equiv[b]g_{1}^{\prime}
and
~\vdash[a]g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}[b]g_{1}^{\prime}
. Then by Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we have that
~\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}^{\prime}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},a,b,g_{1},g_{1}^{\prime})
. Thus
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\sim(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}^{\prime}
. By induction, we have
\mathtt{fn}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})=\mathtt{fn}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}^{\prime})
.

Claim
(1)

We claim that
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})
. Suppose, by contradiction, that
a\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})
. Then

a\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})

\Longrightarrow

a\in\mathtt{fn}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}^{\prime})

(item
2
)

\Longrightarrow

a\in\mathtt{fn}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})

\Longrightarrow

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})

(item
2
)

This leads to a contradiction, because we took
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(g_{1})
and
\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\subseteq\mathtt{Atm}(g_{1})
. This proves the claim.

Claim
(2)

We also claim that
\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})=(a\ b)\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})
. Indeed, from
a,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})
we conclude
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})=\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})
. Thus,

\mathtt{fn}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})=\mathtt{fn}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}^{\prime})

\Longrightarrow

(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})=(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})

(item
2
)

\Longrightarrow

\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})=(({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ a)\circ(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})

\Longrightarrow

\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})=((b\ a)\circ({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ a))\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})

\Longrightarrow

\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})=(a\ b)\cdot((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime}))

\Longrightarrow

\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})=(a\ b)\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})

This finish the prove of the second claim.

Now, let’s prove
\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\}=\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})\setminus\{b\}
. To show this, first observe that from
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})
, we get
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})\setminus\{b\}
and, since
b\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})\setminus\{b\}
, this yields
(a\ b)\cdot(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})\setminus\{b\})=\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})\setminus\{b\}
. Therefore,

\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\}

=

((a\ b)\cdot\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime}))\setminus((a\ b)\cdot\{b\})

by Claim (2)

=

(a\ b)\cdot(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})\setminus\{b\})

=

\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}^{\prime})\setminus\{b\}

This completes the proof that
\mathtt{fn}(g)=\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime})
.

4.

Take an arbitrary but fixed
g\in\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
.

Step 1: Show that
\mathtt{fn}(g)
supports
[g]
.

We will do this by showing, using induction on the structure of
g
, that for any permutation
\pi
, the following holds:

\pi\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(g))\implies\pi\cdot\overline{g}=\overline{g}.

The only interesting case is when
g\equiv[a]g_{1}
. This implies
\mathtt{fn}([a]g_{1})=\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\}
. Then
\pi\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}([a]g_{1}))
implies
\pi(b)=b
for all
b\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\}
.

•

If
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
, then
\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\}=\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
and then
\pi\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}))
which, by induction, implies that
\pi\cdot\overline{g_{1}}=\overline{g_{1}}
. This means that
~\vdash\pi\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{1}
, that is,
\pi\cdot g_{1}\sim g_{1}
.

(a)

\pi(a)=a
: In this case,

\vdash\pi\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{1}

\vdash[\pi(a)]\pi\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}[a]g_{1}

which implies that
\pi\cdot\overline{[a]g_{1}}=\overline{[a]g_{1}}
.

(b)

\pi(a)\neq a
: In this case, the condition
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
implies
\pi(a)\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
by item
3
. Take
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(g_{1})\cup\{a,\pi(a)\}\cup\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
. Then,
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})
and
(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})
are in
{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}))
which, by induction, implies
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\overline{g_{1}}=\overline{g_{1}}
and
(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\overline{g_{1}}=\overline{g_{1}}
, that is,
(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\sim g_{1}
and
g_{1}\sim(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}
. Hence

g_{1}\sim(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}

\Longrightarrow

\pi\cdot g_{1}\sim\pi\cdot((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})

\Longrightarrow

g_{1}\sim(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})

\Longrightarrow

(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\sim(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})

\Longrightarrow

(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})\sim(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}

This implies
~\vdash(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})\approx_{\tt C}(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}
. By an application of rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
, we obtain
\vdash[\pi(a)]\pi\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}[a]g_{1}
and so
\pi\cdot\overline{[a]g_{1}}=\overline{[a]g_{1}}
.

•

If
a\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
, then

(a)

\pi(a)=a
: In this case,
\pi\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}))
. By induction,
\pi\cdot\overline{g_{1}}=\overline{g_{1}}
, i.e.,
~\vdash\pi\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{1}.
The result follows by an application of rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{[a]})
.

(b)

\pi(a)\neq a
: In this case, we claim
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g_{1})
. Suppose, by contradiction, that
a\in\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g_{1})
. Then
a\in\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g_{1})\setminus\{\pi(a)\}
because
\pi(a)\neq a
. This is equivalent to
a\in\pi\cdot(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\})
, which in turn is equivalent to
\pi^{-1}(a)\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\}
. From the hypothesis that
\pi\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}([a]g_{1}))={\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\})
, we get that
\pi(\pi^{-1}(a))=\pi^{-1}(a)
, i.e.,
\pi^{-1}(a)=a
. Applying
\pi
on both sides yields
\pi(a)=a
, a contradiction.

Moreover, we also claim
\pi\cdot g_{1}\sim(\pi(a)\ a)\cdot g_{1}
. By the induction hypothesis, it is sufficient to prove that
(a\ \pi(a))\circ\pi\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1}))
. Since
a\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
, let’s see how
(a\ \pi(a))\circ\pi
interacts with
a
before analysing other atoms in
\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
. Then

((a\ \pi(a))\circ\pi)(a)=(a\ \pi(a))(\pi(a))=a

Now, take
b\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})
. Then

\displaystyle((a\ \pi(a))\circ\pi)(b)

\displaystyle=(a\ \pi(a))(\pi(b))

\displaystyle=\pi(b)

\displaystyle=b

because
\pi\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(g_{1})\setminus\{a\})
. Now, take
c_{1}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(g_{1})\cup\{a,\pi(a)\}\cup\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
. Then,
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in{\tt pfix}(\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g_{1}))
because
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g_{1})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g_{1})
, the latter being a consequence of the fact the
\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot g_{1})\subseteq\mathtt{Atm}(\pi\cdot g_{1})
. By induction, we have
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\overline{\pi\cdot g_{1}}=\overline{\pi\cdot g_{1}}
, that is,
\pi\cdot g_{1}\sim(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})
. Hence,

\pi\cdot g_{1}\sim(\pi(a)\ a)\cdot g_{1}

\Longrightarrow

\pi\cdot g_{1}\sim(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ a)}\cdot g_{1}

\Longrightarrow

(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})\sim(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})

\Longrightarrow

\pi\cdot g_{1}\sim(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})

\Longrightarrow

({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ \pi(a))\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})\sim(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}

\Longrightarrow

(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})\sim(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}

This implies
\vdash(\pi(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot g_{1})\approx_{\tt C}(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}
. By an application of rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
, we obtain
\vdash[\pi(a)]\pi\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}[a]g_{1}
and so
\pi\cdot\overline{[a]g_{1}}=\overline{[a]g_{1}}
.

Step 2: Show that
\mathtt{supp}(g)=\mathtt{fn}(g)
.

Since
\mathtt{fn}(g)
supports
[g]
. By the minimality of the support, we obtain
\mathtt{supp}(g)\subseteq\mathtt{fn}(g)
. It remains to prove the other inclusion:
\mathtt{fn}(g)\subseteq\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})
. Suppose, by contraction, that there exists
a\in\mathtt{fn}(g)
such that
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})
. Choose
b\notin\mathtt{fn}(g)
. Then
b\notin\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})
because we proved that
\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})\subseteq\mathtt{fn}(g)
. Since
a,b\notin\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})
, we have
(a\ b)\cdot\overline{g}=\overline{g}
. Thus,
(a\ b)\cdot g\sim g
. By item
3
, it follows that
\mathtt{fn}((a\ b)\cdot g)=\mathtt{fn}(g)
. Since
b\notin\mathtt{fn}(g)
, it follows by item
2
that
a\notin\mathtt{fn}((a\ b)\cdot g)=\mathtt{fn}(g)
, a contradiction. Therefore, this proves the claim that
\mathtt{supp}([g])=\mathtt{fn}(g)
.

Step 3: Completing the proof.

As a direct consequence of item
3
, we conclude that
\mathtt{supp}(\overline{g})
does not depend on the representative of the class
\overline{g}
, and this completes the proof.

Now, we construct a nominal commutative
\Sigma
-algebra
\mathfrak{F}
as follows:

•

The nominal set
\mathscr{F}=(|\mathscr{F}|,\cdot)
with domain
|\mathscr{F}|=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathtt{C}}
.

•

Define the injective equivariant map
\mathtt{atom}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a)=\overline{a}
, for all
a\in\mathbb{A}
;

•

Define the equivariant map
\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})=\overline{[a]g}
for all
a\in\mathbb{A}
and all
g\in\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
;

•

Define the equivariant map

f^{\mathfrak{F}}(\overline{g_{1}},\ldots,\overline{g_{n}})=\overline{\mathtt{f}(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})}
, for
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
and each
\mathtt{f}:n
in
\Sigma
.

Theorem 0.C.1

\mathfrak{F}
is a nominal model of
\mathtt{C}
.

Proof

The proof is standard and can be found in
[
9
]
.

•

\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathtt{C}}
is a nominal set by Lemma
4
(
1
).

•

The equivariance of each map follows by the fact that
\pi\cdot\overline{g}=\overline{\pi\cdot g}
.

•

It’s easy to see that
\mathtt{atom}^{\mathfrak{F}}
is injective because
\overline{a}=\{a\}
for all
a\in\mathbb{A}
.

•

The following condition is satisfied:
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c}.(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c})\cdot\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})
for all
a\in\mathbb{A}
and all
\overline{g}\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
. Indeed, suppose, by contradiction, that
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c}.(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c})\cdot\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})
does not hold, that is, the set
D_{1}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c}\mid(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c})\cdot\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})\}
is finite. By Theorem
4
, we have
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g}))
. Define
D_{2}=\{a\}\cup\mathtt{supp}(\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g}))
. Then, for any atom
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin D_{2}
, it follows that
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})=\mathtt{abs}^{\mathfrak{F}}(a,\overline{g})
. Therefore,
\mathbb{A}\setminus D_{2}\subseteq D_{1}
, which is a contradiction with the fact that
D_{1}
is finite.

•

Given
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathbb{F}(\Sigma\cup\mathsf{D})
. For each
\mathtt{f^{C}}
in
\Sigma
, note that we have

(refl)

~\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{1}

(refl)

~\vdash g_{2}\approx_{\tt C}g_{2}

({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}})

~\vdash\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{1},g_{2})\approx_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{2},g_{1})

Thus,
\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{1},g_{2})\sim\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{2},g_{1})
and hence
\overline{\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{1},g_{2})}=\overline{\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{2},g_{1})}
. Consequently,
f^{\mathtt{C},\mathfrak{F}}(\overline{g_{1}},\overline{g_{2}})=f^{\mathtt{C},\mathfrak{F}}(\overline{g_{2}},\overline{g_{1}})
, proving the result.

\mathfrak{F}
will be called the
free-term model of
\mathtt{C}
.

Useful auxiliary results.

Below, we present some results from nominal set theory that will be useful:

•

If
\mathscr{X}
is a nominal set, then the powerset
\mathcal{P}(\mathscr{X})
with the action
\pi\cdot S=\{\pi\cdot x\mid x\in S\}
, is not necessarily a nominal set. However, the restriction of
\mathcal{P}(\mathscr{X})
,

\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathscr{X})=\{S\subseteq\mathscr{X}\mid\text{$S$ is finitely supported}\},

equipped with the same action, is a nominal set.

•

Suppose
S
is a set, all of whose elements have finite support. If
\bigcup\{\mathtt{supp}(x)\mid x\in S\}
is finite then
\mathtt{supp}(S)
exists and

\mathtt{supp}(S)=\bigcup\{\mathtt{supp}(x)\mid x\in S\}.

The proof can be found in
[
16
]
.

Lemma 5

For all natural
n\geq 1
, the nominal set
\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})
is a model of
\mathtt{C}
when equipped with the following structure:

1.

\mathtt{atom}(a)=\{(a,\ldots,a)\}
for all
a\in\mathbb{A}

2.

\mathtt{abs}(a,S)=S\setminus\{x\in S\mid a\in\mathtt{supp}(x)\}
for all
a\in\mathbb{A}
and all
S\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})

3.

Every
\mathtt{f}:n
is mapped to
f(S_{1},\ldots,S_{n})=S_{1}\cup\ldots\cup S_{n}
for all
S_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})
.

Proof

It’s not difficult to see that:

•

all mappings are equivariant.

•

\mathtt{atom}
is injective.

•

\mathtt{abs}
satisfies
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c}.(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c})\cdot\mathtt{abs}(a,S)=\mathtt{abs}(a,S)
for all
a\in\mathbb{A}
and
S\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})
. This is a consequence of Pitts’ equivalence and
a\notin\mathtt{supp}(\mathtt{abs}(a,S))
by construction.

•

Each
\mathtt{f^{C}}:n
in the signature is mapped to
f(S_{1},S_{2})=S_{1}\cup S_{2}
and
f(S_{1},S_{2})=f(S_{2},S_{1})
for all
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})
. Thus, it is indeed a model of
\mathtt{C}
.

Lemma 6

The following hold:

1.

If
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
, then
\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\subseteq\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
.

2.

If
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
, then
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#})\right)
.

Proof

1.

If
\pi=\mathtt{id}
, then the result follows trivially. Assume that
\pi\neq\mathtt{id}
. Suppose, by contradiction, that there is an atom
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
such that
a\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
. Consider the algebra
\mathfrak{A}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fin}}(\mathbb{A})
and the valuation
\varsigma
given by
\varsigma(X)=\{a\}
and
\varsigma(Y)=\{b\}
for all
Y\not\equiv X
, where
b
is fresh. Then, it is not difficult to see that
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is valid. By hypothesis, this means that
\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
, i.e.,
\pi(a)=a
, which in turn implies that
a\notin\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
, a contradiction.

2.

Let’s start proving the following claims

Claim
(1)

We claim that
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\emptyset
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\emptyset
. In fact,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
means that for all models
\mathfrak{A}
and all valuations
\varsigma
,

If
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is valid, then
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
.

(6)

\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
being valid, by definition, means that
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\pi^{\prime}\cdot\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
holds for all
\pi^{\prime}\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})_{\#}
. By generalized Pitts’s equivalence (Lemma
2
) we have, for all
\pi^{\prime}\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})_{\#}
, that

•

\mathtt{dom}((\pi^{\prime})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\emptyset
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap(\bigcup\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}))=\emptyset
. (note that
\pi^{\prime}=(\pi^{\prime})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
for all
\pi^{\prime}\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})_{\#}
.)

By (
6
), we have
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
holds for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\emptyset
. As a consequence of Lemma
1
, we have
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\emptyset
.

Claim
(2)

We claim that
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\subseteq\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#})
. Suppose, by contradiction, that there is an atom
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
such that
a\notin\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#})
. Then
a\in\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\setminus{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
by item
1
. Consider the valuation
\varsigma^{*}
given by
\varsigma^{*}(X)=\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\setminus{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
and
\varsigma^{*}(Y)=\{b\}
for all
Y\not\equiv X
, where
b
is fresh. Thus,
\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\setminus{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
and
\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})=\{b\}
for all
Y\not\equiv X
. Moreover, it’s not hard to see that
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fin}}(\mathbb{A})}_{\varsigma^{*}}
is valid. Therefore,
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma})
, which is a contradiction with Claim (1).

As a consequence of Claim (2), we have that
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#})\right)
.

The next lemma is the most challenging as it establishes a characterisation of the permutations on semantic judgements of the form
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
, which by definition, are the judgements that are valid in every model and under every valuation: It must be the case that the permutation is generated by the permutations in the context.

Lemma 7
(Characterisation of valid fixed-points)

If
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
, then
\pi\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
.

Proof

If
\pi=\mathtt{id}
, then the result follows trivially. Assume that
\pi\neq\mathtt{id}
. Here are some useful information:

•

By generalized Pitts’ equivalence (Lemma
2
), from
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
.

•

By Lemma
6
(
2
), we have
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#})\right)
.

•

By Lemma
6
(
1
),
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\subseteq\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\setminus{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
.

•

To simplify the proof we abbreviate
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})={\mathcal{G}}\left((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#}\right)\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
.

Then, it is sufficient to prove that
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
. Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
be a list enumerating all atoms being mentioned in all permutations of
(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
. Then
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
where
\mathbb{A}^{n}
is the
n
-ary Cartesian power of the nominal set
\mathbb{A}
. The set
\mathbb{A}^{n}
is a nominal set when equipped with the usual pointwise action. The orbit of
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
over
\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
, is the set
{\cal O}=\{\pi\cdot(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\mid\pi\in\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle\}
.
Since every element of
{\cal O}
has finite support and
\bigcup_{x\in{\cal O}}\mathtt{supp}(x)=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\}
, it follows that
{\cal O}
itself has finite support and
\mathtt{supp}({\cal O})=\bigcup_{x\in{\cal O}}\mathtt{supp}(x)
. This proves that
{\cal O}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})
. Take the model
\mathfrak{A}
as
\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fs}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})
and the valuation

\varsigma(Y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}{\cal O},&Y\equiv X\\
\{(d_{Y},\ldots,d_{Y})\}&Y\not\equiv X\end{array}\right.

where
d_{Y}
is a fresh atom for
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{Y}
.

•

For every
\rho\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
such that
Y\not\equiv X
, it holds that
\mathtt{dom}(\rho)\cap\mathtt{supp}(\varsigma(Y))=\emptyset
. Consequently, by the definition of support, we have
\rho\cdot\varsigma(Y)=\varsigma(Y)
and so
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\rho\cdot\varsigma(Y)=\varsigma(Y).

For those
\rho\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})_{\#}
, we have that
\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}=\rho
, and given that
\rho\cdot\varsigma(Y)=\varsigma(Y)
is true for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
satisfying
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\bigcup_{Y}\mathtt{supp}(\varsigma(Y))=\emptyset
, it follows, by the generalized Pitts’ equivalence (Lemma
2
), that
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\rho\cdot\varsigma(Y)=\varsigma(Y)
.

•

Now, for those
\rho\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon_{\#}
, we have that
\mathtt{dom}(\rho)\cap\mathtt{supp}(\cal O)=\emptyset
and hence by the same reason, we have
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\rho\cdot\varsigma(X)=\varsigma(X)
.

•

By the definition of
{\cal O}
, we have that
\rho\cdot{\cal O}={\cal O}
holds for all
(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
. In particular,
\rho\cdot{\cal O}={\cal O}
for all permutation
\rho
such that
\rho\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
. So
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\rho\cdot\varsigma(X)=\varsigma(X)
.

Consequently,
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is valid.

From
\Upsilon\vDash\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}X
, we have
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot{\cal O}={\cal O}
. Then for all element
x\in{\cal O}
there is an element
y\in{\cal O}
such that
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x=y
. By the definition of
{\cal O}
,
x=\pi_{x}\cdot(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
and
y=\pi_{y}\cdot(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
where
\pi_{x},\pi_{y}\in\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
. Hence
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot x=y
implies
\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot(\pi_{x}\cdot(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}))=\pi_{y}\cdot(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
, leading to

(\pi_{y}^{-1}\circ\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\circ\pi_{x})\cdot(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}).

This implies
(\pi_{y}^{-1}\circ\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\circ\pi_{x})(a_{i})=a_{i}
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
and thus
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{y}^{-1}\circ\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\circ\pi_{x})\cap\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\}=\emptyset.

As observed at the beginning, we have
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\subseteq\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. Then the only possibility is
\pi_{y}^{-1}\circ\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\circ\pi_{x}=\mathtt{id}\in\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
. Therefore,

\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}=\pi_{y}\circ(\pi_{y}^{-1}\circ\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\circ\pi_{x})\circ\pi_{x}^{-1}\in\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle,

Since
\pi=\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\circ\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
, we obtain
\pi\in{\mathcal{G}}\left((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#}\right)\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
.

Lemma 8

If
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash t\approx_{\tt C}u
and
t
is a suspension, i.e.,
t\equiv\pi_{1}\cdot X
, then
u
must be a suspension, i.e.,
u\equiv\pi_{2}\cdot X
, for some
\pi_{2}
.

Proof

Just observe that for each
u\not\equiv\pi_{2}\cdot X
, it is possible to find an algebra
\mathfrak{A}
and a valuation
\varsigma
that validate the context, but
\llbracket\pi_{1}\cdot X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}\neq\llbracket u\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
. Here, we will analyse only the case where
u\equiv\pi_{2}\cdot Y
.

Take
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
. Consider the algebra
\mathfrak{A}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathtt{fin}}(\mathbb{A})
with the valuation
\varsigma
defined by
\varsigma(X)=\{a_{1},a_{2}\},\varsigma(Y)=\{a_{1},a_{3}\}
, and
\varsigma(Z)=\emptyset
for all
Z\not\equiv X,Y
. Then
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}
is clearly valid but

\pi_{1}\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}=\{a_{1},a_{2}\}\neq\{a_{1},a_{3}\}=\pi_{2}\cdot\llbracket Y\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{A}}_{\varsigma}.

Proof of Completeness.

The rest of the section will be based on the assumption that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash t\approx_{\tt C}u
.

Definition 10

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the atoms in
\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t,u)
. Let
\mathcal{X}
be the variables mentioned anywhere in
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t
or
u
.

1.

For each
X\in\mathcal{X}
pick the following data:

•

let
{\cal A}_{X}
be the set of atoms
a\in{\cal A}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
such that there is no
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\#}
with
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
.

•

let
a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}
be the atoms in
{\cal A}_{X}
in some arbitrary but fixed order.

•

let
\mathtt{d}_{X}:k_{X}
be a fresh term-former.

2.

For each
X\notin\mathcal{X}
, let
\mathtt{d}_{X}:0
be a term-former. Let
\sf{D}
be the set of all
\mathtt{d}
’s (so for each
X\in\mathbb{V}
we have a
\mathtt{d}_{X}\in\sf{D}
).

Definition 11

Let
\sigma
be the following substitution:

X\sigma\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})&(X\in\mathcal{X})\\
\mathtt{d}_{X}()&(X\notin\mathcal{X})\end{array}\right.

Thus,
\sigma
maps all variables mentioned in
t
or
u
to an appropriate ground term such that the support we know.

Let
\Sigma^{+}=\Sigma\cup\sf{D}
and consider
\mathbb{F}(\Sigma^{+})
the set of free (nominal) terms. For each
X\in{\cal X}
, define
R_{X}
as the set of all identities of the following form:

\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})\approx\pi\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}),

for all
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
. Let
R:=\bigcup_{X\in{\cal X}}R_{X}
. Denote by
{\cal R}
the equivariant equivalence closure of
R
. Define the relation
{\cal E}
by

(g_{1},g_{2})\in{\cal E}\text{ iff }\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{2}\text{ or }(g_{1},g_{2})\in{\cal R}.

Lemma 9

{\cal E}
is an equivariant equivalence relation on
\overline{\mathbb{F}}(\Sigma^{+})
.

Proof

1.

Reflexivity
,
Symmetry
and
Equivariance
follows because both relations
\approx_{\tt C}
and
{\cal R}
are reflexive, symmetric and equivariant.

2.

Transitivity
: Suppose

(g_{1},g_{2})\in{\cal E}
and
(g_{2},g_{3})\in{\cal E}
.

(a)

If
~\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{2}
, then we have two cases:

•

Case
~\vdash g_{2}\approx_{\tt C}g_{3}
. In this case,
~\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{3}
follows by the fact that
\approx_{\tt C}
is transitive and so
(g_{1},g_{3})\in{\cal E}
.

•

Case
(g_{2},g_{3})\in{\cal R}
. In this case,
g_{2}
is of the form

g_{2}\equiv\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}^{\prime})

which, by
~\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{2}
forces
g_{1}\equiv g_{2}
. Thus,
(g_{1},g_{3})\in{\cal E}
.

(b)

If
(g_{1},g_{2})\in{\cal R}
, then we have two cases:

•

Case
~\vdash g_{2}\approx_{\tt C}g_{3}
. This case is similar to the second bullet of the previous case (a).

•

Case
(g_{2},g_{3})\in{\cal R}
. In this case,
(g_{1},g_{3})\in{\cal R}
follows by the fact that
{\cal R}
is transitive and hence
(g_{1},g_{3})\in{\cal E}
.

As a consequence, the set
\mathbb{F}(\Sigma^{+})/{{\cal E}}
forms a nominal set, which we denote by
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\cal E}
. This set satisfies properties analogous to those stated in Lemma
4
. The proof follows a very similar structure, with the only difference lying in item
3
.

Lemma 10

If
g^{\prime}\in\overline{g}
, then
\mathtt{fn}(g)=\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime})
.

Proof

Suppose
g^{\prime}\in\overline{g}
. Then
(g,g^{\prime})\in{\cal E}
, so
~\vdash g\approx_{\tt C}g^{\prime}
or
(g,g^{\prime})\in{\cal R}
. If
~\vdash g\approx_{\tt C}g^{\prime}
, then the proof is the same as the one in Lemma
4
(
3
). If
(g,g^{\prime})\in{\cal R}
, then we have two possibilities:

•

g\equiv\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})
.

In this case,
g^{\prime}\equiv g
or
g^{\prime}\equiv\pi\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})
for some permutation
\pi
such that
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
. In both scenarios,
\mathtt{fn}(g)=\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime})
, as
\pi
merely rearranges some of the atoms in
a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}
.

•

g\equiv\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}^{\prime})
,

In this case,

g\equiv\mu\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})

where
\mu=(a_{X_{1}}\ a_{X_{1}}^{\prime})\ldots(a_{X_{k_{X}}}\ a_{X_{k_{X}}}^{\prime})
. Then
g^{\prime}\equiv g
or

g^{\prime}\equiv\mu\cdot(\pi\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}))

for some permutation
\pi
such that
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathtt{fn}(g)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fn}(\mu\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}))

\displaystyle=\mu\cdot\mathtt{fn}(\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})

\displaystyle=\mu\cdot\mathtt{fn}(\pi\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}))

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fn}(\mu\cdot(\pi\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})))

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fn}(g^{\prime}).

Additionally, the set
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\cal E}
, equipped with the same equivariant maps defined in Subsection
0.C.2
, forms an algebra that is a model of
\mathtt{C}
. We denote this algebra by
\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}
.

Define
\varsigma^{*}
on
\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}
by
\varsigma^{*}(X)=\overline{X\sigma}
for every
X\in\mathbb{V}
.

Lemma 11

The following hold:

1.

\llbracket t\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\overline{t\sigma}
and
\llbracket u\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\overline{u\sigma}
.

2.

\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}
is valid.

Proof

1.

Direct by induction on the structure of
t
and
u
. To illustrate, consider the case
t\equiv\pi\cdot X
.

\displaystyle\llbracket\pi\cdot X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\pi\cdot\varsigma(X)=\pi\cdot\overline{X\sigma}=\overline{\pi\cdot(X\sigma)}=\overline{(\pi\cdot X)\sigma}.

2.

Let
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
. Then
X\in{\cal X}
.

•

Suppose
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})_{\#}
. Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
be such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}})=\emptyset
. By construction
\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\cap{\cal A}_{X}=\emptyset
so
\mathtt{dom}(\pi)\cap\mathtt{supp}(\varsigma^{*}(X))=\emptyset
which, by the definition of support, gives us
\pi\cdot\varsigma^{*}(X)\equiv\varsigma^{*}(X)
. Consequently. we have
\pi\cdot\overline{\varsigma^{*}(X)}=\overline{\varsigma^{*}(X)}
, that is,
\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}
. This proves that the set
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\mid\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}\}
is cofinite. Thus,
\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}
.

•

Suppose
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
. By construction,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})}

\displaystyle=\overline{\pi\cdot\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})}

\displaystyle=\pi\cdot\overline{\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})},

so
\pi\cdot\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}}
holds. Since this holds for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cap\mathtt{supp}(\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}_{\cal E}}_{\varsigma^{*}})=\emptyset
, the result follows.

Let
\Pi
be a proof of the judgment
~\vdash t\sigma\approx_{\tt C}u\sigma
. By the definition of
\sigma
, the terms
t\sigma
and
u\sigma
belong to
\mathbb{F}(\Sigma^{+})
, and the subterm
X\sigma
occurs in the position where
X
occurs in
t
or
u
. Now, we aim to find a method to reverse
t\sigma
or
u\sigma
into terms in
\Sigma
, in such a way that it allows us to recover a derivation of
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
.

Definition 12

Let
\mathcal{A}^{+}
be
\mathcal{A}
extended with:

•

a set
\mathcal{C}
of atoms mentioned anywhere in
\Pi
(that were not already in
\mathcal{A}
).

•

a set
\mathcal{B}
of fresh atoms, in bijection with
\mathcal{A}
— for convenience, we fix a bijection and write
b_{X_{i}}
for the atom corresponding under that bijection with
a_{X_{i}}
— and

•

one fresh atom
e
(so
e
does not occur in
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}
or
\mathcal{B}
).

{\cal A}^{+}={\cal A\cup C\cup B}\cup\{e\}.

Let
{\cal B}_{X}
be the set of
b_{X_{i}}
’s corresponding with
a_{X_{i}}
’s. Denote by
({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})
the permutation
(a_{X_{1}}\ b_{X_{1}})\circ\ldots\circ(a_{X_{k_{X}}}\ b_{X_{k_{X}}})
.

Definition 13

Define
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}
to be
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
extended with the following constraints:

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup\{c^{*}\}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{*}}
is a fresh name.

•

({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}^{*}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}^{*}}\ \ldots\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{n}^{*}}\ e\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{*}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}X
for all
X\in{\cal X}
, where
{\cal C}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}^{*}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{n}^{*}}\}
.

•

\pi^{({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})}\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y
for all
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in\Upsilon_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}
.

Definition 14

For the rest of this subsection let
g
and
h
range over ground terms in
\Sigma^{+}
that mention only atoms from
\mathcal{A}^{+}\setminus\mathcal{B}\cup\{e\}
. Define an
inverse mapping

(-)^{-1}
from such ground terms to terms in
\Sigma
inductively as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}a^{-1}&\equiv&a\\
\mathtt{d}_{X}()^{-1}&\equiv&e\\
\mathtt{f}(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})^{-1}&\equiv&\mathtt{f}(g_{1}^{-1},\ldots,g_{n}^{-1})\\
([a]g)^{-1}&\equiv&[a]g^{-1}\\
\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{\scalebox{.4}{$X$}}}}^{\prime})^{-1}&\equiv&\pi_{X}(a_{X_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{\scalebox{.4}{$X$}}}}^{\prime})\cdot X\end{array}

where
\pi_{X}(a_{X_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{\scalebox{.4}{$X$}}}}^{\prime})=(a_{X_{1}}^{\prime}\ b_{X_{1}})\circ\ldots\circ(a_{X_{k_{\scalebox{.4}{$X$}}}}^{\prime}\ b_{X_{k_{\scalebox{.4}{$X$}}}})\circ(b_{X_{1}}\ a_{X_{1}})\circ\ldots\circ(b_{X_{k_{\scalebox{.4}{$X$}}}}\ a_{X_{k_{\scalebox{.4}{$X$}}}}).

Lemma 12

\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})^{-1}\equiv X
.

Proof

Consequence of
\pi_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})=\mathtt{id}
.

The inverse mapping is equivariant (for the terms we care about):

Lemma 13

If
\pi\in\bigcap_{X\in\mathtt{Var}(g^{-1})}{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#})\cup{\cal C}\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle\mathtt{perm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\right\rangle
, then
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(\pi\cdot g)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}\pi\cdot g^{-1}
.

Proof

The proof is by induction on the structure of
g
. The only non-trivial case is when
g\equiv\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}}^{\prime})
. To prove
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(\pi\cdot g)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}\pi\cdot g^{-1}
, we must show that
(\pi^{-1})^{({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})^{-1}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})
. This is equivalent to prove that
\pi^{({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})
because
({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})^{-1}=({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})
. In this case,
\mathtt{Var}(g^{-1})=\{X\}
and so
\pi\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#})\cup{\cal C}\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle\mathtt{perm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\right\rangle
. To simply notation, let’s call it just
{\mathcal{G}}\left((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#}\cup{\cal C}\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
. Applying
({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})
on both sides, we get
\pi^{({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#}\cup{\cal C}\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}^{({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle
. Since

•

\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})={\mathcal{G}}\left((\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#}\cup{\cal C}\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}^{+}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle

•

{\mathcal{G}}\left((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#}\cup{\cal C}\right)\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}\left((\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#}\cup{\cal C}\right)

•

\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}^{({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle\subseteq\left\langle(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}^{+}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}\right\rangle

it follows that
\pi^{({\cal A}_{X}\ {\cal B}_{X})}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})
.

Lemma 14

The following hold:

1.

\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(t\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}t
and
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(u\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}u
.

2.

If
~\vdash t\sigma\approx_{\tt C}u\sigma
, then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
.

Proof

1.

The proof is by induction on
t
. The only interesting case is when
t\equiv\pi_{1}\cdot X
. First note that

•

X\sigma\in\mathbb{F}(\Sigma^{+})
, and
X\sigma
only involves atoms from
\mathcal{A}^{+}\setminus\mathcal{B}\cup\{e\}
.

•

By Lemma
8
,
t\equiv\pi_{2}\cdot X
. Since
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi_{1}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X
if and only if
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash(\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1})\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X
, we may assume w.l.o.g. that
s\equiv\pi\cdot X
and
t\equiv X
.

•

By Lemma
12
,
(X\sigma)^{-1}\equiv X
and so
\mathtt{Var}((X\sigma)^{-1})=\{X\}
.

•

By Lemma
7
, we get

\pi\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#})\cup{\cal C}\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle\mathtt{perm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\right\rangle

Applying Lemma
13
, we conclude that
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(\pi\cdot(X\sigma))^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}\pi\cdot(X\sigma)^{-1}
. Finally, by applying Lemma
12
once more, and using that
(\pi\cdot X)\sigma\equiv\pi\cdot(X\sigma)
, we derive
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash((\pi\cdot X)\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}\pi\cdot X
.

2.

Firstly, let’s prove the following claim: If
~\vdash s\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma
then
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(s\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}(t\sigma)^{-1}
.

The proof is by induction on the last rule used to obtain the derivation
~\vdash s\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma
.

•

If the last rule applied is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{a})
, then by Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)),
s\sigma\equiv a\equiv t\sigma
. Consequently,
(a\sigma)^{-1}\equiv a\equiv(t\sigma)^{-1}
. Therefore, the derivation
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(a\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}(a\sigma)^{-1}
follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{a})
.

•

If the last rule applied is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f}})
, then by Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)),
s\sigma\equiv\mathtt{f}(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})
,
t\sigma\equiv\mathtt{f}(g_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,g_{n}^{\prime})
,
and
\vdash g_{i}\approx_{\tt C}g_{i}^{\prime}
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
.
By the construction of
\sigma
, it forces
s\equiv\mathtt{f}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})
and
t\equiv\mathtt{f}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})
because the substitution does not change the structure of the term
s
and
t
. Thus,
s_{i}\sigma\equiv g_{i}
and
t_{i}\sigma\equiv g_{i}^{\prime}
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
. Then, for each
i=1,\ldots,n
, the derivation
\vdash g_{i}\approx_{\tt C}g_{i}^{\prime}
become
\vdash s_{i}\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t_{i}\sigma
. By induction, we have that
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(s_{i}\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}(t_{i}\sigma)^{-1}
. Then, by an application of the rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f}})
, we obtain
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(\mathtt{f}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}(\mathtt{f}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})\sigma)^{-1}
.

•

If the last rule applied is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}})
