, then the it follows similarly to the previous case.

In this case, by Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)),
s\sigma\equiv\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{0},g_{1})
and
t\sigma\equiv\mathtt{f^{C}}(g_{0}^{\prime},g_{1}^{\prime})
, and

–

either
\vdash g_{0}\approx_{\tt C}g_{0}^{\prime}
and
\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{1}^{\prime}

This case is analogous to the case of the rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f}})
.

–

or
\vdash g_{0}\approx_{\tt C}g_{1}^{\prime}
and
\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{0}^{\prime}
.

This case is also analogous to the case of the rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f}})
, but we will prove it anyway.

By the construction of
\sigma
, we conclude that
s\equiv\mathtt{f^{C}}(s_{0},s_{1})
and
t\equiv\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
and, for
i=0,1
,
s_{i}\sigma\equiv g_{i}
and
t_{i}\sigma\equiv g_{i}^{\prime}
. Thus,
\vdash g_{0}\approx_{\tt C}g_{1}^{\prime}
and and
\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{0}^{\prime}
becomes, respectively,
\vdash s_{0}\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}\sigma
and
\vdash s_{1}\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}\sigma
. The result follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}})
.

•

If the last rule applied is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{[a]})
, then by Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)),
s\sigma\equiv[a]g_{1},t\sigma\equiv[a]g_{2}
, and
~\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{2}
. Again, the definition of
\sigma
forces
s\equiv[a]s^{\prime}
and
t\equiv[a]t^{\prime}
, which in turn yields
s^{\prime}\sigma\equiv g_{1}
and
t^{\prime}\sigma\equiv g_{2}
. Thus,
~\vdash g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}g_{2}
becomes
~\vdash s^{\prime}\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t^{\prime}\sigma
. By induction,
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash s^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t^{\prime}
and the result follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{[a]})
.

•

If the last rule applied is
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
, then by Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)),
s\sigma\equiv[a]g_{1},t\sigma\equiv[b]g_{2}
, and
~\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin a,b,g_{1},g_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}
.

The definition of
\sigma
forces
s\equiv[a]s^{\prime}
and
t\equiv[b]t^{\prime}
, which in turn yields
s^{\prime}\sigma\equiv g_{1}
and
t^{\prime}\sigma\equiv g_{2}
. Thus,

\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2}

\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(s^{\prime}\sigma)\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(t^{\prime}\sigma)

\vdash((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s^{\prime})\sigma\approx_{\tt C}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime})\sigma

By induction, we get

\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime},c_{1}}}\vdash(((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot s^{\prime})\sigma)^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t^{\prime})\sigma)^{-1}

\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime},c_{1}}}\vdash((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(s^{\prime}\sigma))^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(t^{\prime}\sigma))^{-1}

\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime},c_{1}}}\vdash((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2})^{-1}

Observe that
\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})=\mathtt{Var}(g_{2}^{-1})
. If
\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})\neq\mathtt{Var}(g_{2}^{-1})
, let’s say
Z\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})
and
Z\notin\mathtt{Var}(g_{2}^{-1})
. By the definition of
\sigma
and
-^{-1}
, this means that
\mathtt{d}_{Z}(a_{Z_{1}},\ldots,a_{Z_{k_{Z}}})
occurs in
g_{1}
at the same position that
Z
occurs in
g_{1}^{-1}
. However, since
~\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2}
, this means that
\mathtt{d}_{Z}(a_{Z_{1}},\ldots,a_{Z_{k_{Z}}})
must occur in
g_{2}
at the same position that it occurs in
g_{1}
. This implies that
Z\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{2}^{-1})
, which is a contradiction.

We claim that
a\notin{\cal A}_{X}
for every
X\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})
. Suppose, by contradiction, that
a\in{\cal A}_{X}
for some
X\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})
.

From
~\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2}
, we have
\mathtt{fn}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})=\mathtt{fn}((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2})
. This implies
a\notin\mathtt{fn}(g_{2})
.

Since
\mathtt{Var}(g_{2}^{-1})=\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})
, it follows that
X\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{2}^{-1})
and hence
a\in\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})
and
\mathtt{d}_{X}(a_{X_{1}},\ldots,a_{X_{k_{X}}})
occurs in
g_{2}
. Then
a\in\mathtt{fn}(g_{2})
, a contradiction.

Since
a\notin{\cal A}_{X}
for every
X\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})
. By definition this means that for every
X\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})
there is some
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon_{\#}|_{X}
such that
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
. Thus

(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in\bigcap_{X\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{1}^{-1})}{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{\#}|_{X})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime},c_{1}}\cup{\cal C}\right)\left\langle\Upsilon_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}}|_{X}\right\rangle

By Lemma
13
, we obtain the derivation
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime},c_{1}}}\vdash((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1})^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}^{-1}
.

Similarly, we have
b\notin{\cal A}_{X}
for every
X\in\mathtt{Var}(g_{2}^{-1})
and consequently
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime},c_{1}}}\vdash((b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2})^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2}^{-1}
. Therefore,
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime},c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{1}^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}(b\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot g_{2}^{-1}
follows by transitivity. Therefore, the derivation
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash[a]g_{1}^{-1}\approx_{\tt C}[b]g_{2}^{-1}
follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab}).

Completing the proof

Now that we proved the claim, let’s proceed with the proof of the lemma: by Lemma
1
, symmetry and transitivity of
\approx_{\tt C}
, we deduce
\Upsilon^{+}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash s\approx_{\tt C}t
and by Strengthening (Theorem
4.1
(
4
)) and Lemma
17
, we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash s\approx_{\tt C}t
.

With all of this established, we can finally prove Completeness.

Proof
(of Completeness)

Suppose
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash t\approx_{\tt C}u
, so
\llbracket\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u\rrbracket^{\mathscr{F}}_{\varsigma^{*}}
is valid. We want to show that there is a derivation of
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
. In fact, by Lemma
11
(
2
) we have
\llbracket t\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\llbracket u\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}}_{\varsigma^{*}}
. By Lemma
14
(
1
),
\llbracket t\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\overline{t\sigma}
and
\llbracket u\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{F}}_{\varsigma^{*}}=\overline{u\sigma}
. Therefore
\overline{t\sigma}=\overline{u\sigma}
, that is,
~\vdash t\sigma\approx_{\tt C}u\sigma
. It follows by Lemma
14
(
2
) that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
, as desired.

Appendix 0.D
Proof of Preservation by substitution

The next results aim to prove the property of Preservation by substitution. We begin establishing by establishing a lemma stating that fix-point constraints are closed under composition and inverses.

Lemma 15

1.

If
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{2}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
, then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash(\pi_{1}\circ\pi_{2})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
.

2.

If
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
, then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi^{-1}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
.

Proof

Direct consequence of Equivariance and Equivalence in Theorem
4.1
.

The following lemma formalizes in the calculus the valid result in nominal set theory, which states that any swapping
(a\ b)
of two fresh names
a
and
b
for an element
x
fixes the element
x
.

Lemma 16

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t)
. Then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t\approx_{\tt C}t
.

Proof

The proof is direct by induction on the structure of
t
. Here we show only the case when
t\equiv\pi\cdot X
. We aim to prove
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot(\pi\cdot X)\approx_{\tt C}(\pi\cdot X)
. Given that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\pi)
, we have

\pi^{-1}\circ({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ\pi=({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}|_{X}).

Therefore, the result follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

The next lemma provides a method for handling vacuous quantifications in a derivation.

Lemma 17

Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
. If
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
. The converse holds if
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t,u)
.

Proof

By induction on the last rule applied to obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash t\approx_{\tt C}u
. For instance, if the last rule is
({{\approx}}_{\tt\,}\mathbf{var})
, then we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), it follows that
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
, we have
\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})\subseteq\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
. Then
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
and so the result follows by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

The following lemma formalizes a fundamental property of nominal sets, stating that if a permutation’s domain contains only fresh names for an element
x
, then
x
is fixed by that permutation.

Lemma 18

Let
I
be a non-empty, finite set of indices, and let
\{\pi_{i}\mid i\in I\}
be a set of permutations. Suppose
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
for all
a\in\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathtt{dom}((\pi_{i})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\right)\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t,\{\pi_{i}\mid i\in I\})
. Then,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi^{\prime}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
for all
\pi^{\prime}
such that
\mathtt{dom}(\pi^{\prime})\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathtt{dom}((\pi_{i})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\right)\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
.

Proof

We proceed by induction on the number of disjoint cycles in the decomposition of
\pi^{\prime}
.

Base case.

Suppose
\pi^{\prime}
is a single cycle, say
\pi^{\prime}=(a_{1}\ a_{2}\ \ldots\ a_{m})
, which can be expressed as product of swappings:

\pi^{\prime}=(a_{1}\ a_{m})\circ(a_{1}\ a_{m-1})\circ(a_{1}\ a_{3})\circ(a_{1}\ a_{2})

For each
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
:
(a_{1}\ a_{k})=(a_{1}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\circ(a_{k}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\circ(a_{1}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}).
By the assumption in the statement, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a_{k}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
for all
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
. By Lemma
15
(
1
), we have

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a_{1}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\circ(a_{k}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\circ(a_{1}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t

which implies
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a_{1}\ a_{k})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. By applying Lemma
15
(
1
) again, we obtain:

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a_{1}\ a_{m})\circ(a_{1}\ a_{m-1})\circ(a_{1}\ a_{3})\circ(a_{1}\ a_{2})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t.

which is the same as
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a_{1}\ a_{2}\ \ldots\ a_{m})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. By Lemma
17
, we conclude that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi^{\prime}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
.

Inductive step:

Now, assume the result holds for all permutations
\pi^{\prime}
(under the conditions of the statement) that decompose into
n-1
disjoint cycles. Consider a permutation
\pi^{\prime}
such that
\mathtt{dom}(\pi^{\prime})\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathtt{dom}((\pi_{i})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\right)\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
and consisting of
n
disjoint cycles:
\pi^{\prime}=\eta_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\eta_{n}.
Since
\mathtt{dom}(\eta_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\eta_{n-1})\subseteq\mathtt{dom}(\pi^{\prime})
, it follows:

\mathtt{dom}(\eta_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\eta_{n-1})\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathtt{dom}((\pi_{i})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\right)\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.

By the induction hypothesis, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(\eta_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\eta_{n-1})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t.
Using a similar argument to the base case, we show that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash\eta_{n}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. By Lemma
15
(
1
), it follows that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash\pi^{\prime}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
which, by Lemma
17
, yields
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi^{\prime}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
.

The following lemma describes a particular derivation case, which will be useful for establishing the next result.

Lemma 19

Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},a,\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1}))
. Then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
iff
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t_{0}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t_{1}
.

Proof

(\Leftarrow)

This case follows by applying the rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}})
.

(\Rightarrow)

The proof follows by an analysis on every possible pair of terms
(t_{0},t_{1})
. Since
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
iff
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{1},t_{0})
holds, it’s sufficient to analyse only half of the possibilities. The only non-trivial cases are the cases where
t_{0}
and
t_{1}
are either both suspensions or both abstractions.

1.

t_{0}\equiv\pi\cdot X
and
t_{1}\equiv\pi^{\prime}\cdot Y
.

In this case,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(\pi\cdot X,\pi^{\prime}\cdot Y)
implies
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash\mathtt{f^{C}}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot X),(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi^{\prime}\cdot Y))\approx_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(\pi\cdot X,\pi^{\prime}\cdot Y).

(a)

Y\not\equiv X
.

By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), the valid branch is:
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot X)\approx_{\tt C}\pi\cdot X
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi^{\prime}\cdot Y)\approx_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot Y
, which are precisely the derivations we wanted.

(b)

Y\equiv X
.

By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), there are two possible branches:

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot X)\approx_{\tt C}\pi\cdot X
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi^{\prime}\cdot X)\approx_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot X
, and the result follows trivially.

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi\cdot X)\approx_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot X
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot(\pi^{\prime}\cdot X)\approx_{\tt C}\pi\cdot X
.

Note that, by the Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)) and the symmetry of
\approx_{\tt C}
, these two derivations are equivalent, so it’s enough to focus on just one. We will work with the first one. Applying Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we obtain:
\pi^{\prime-1}\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\circ\pi\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
. To simplify the proof, let’s call
\gamma:=\pi^{\prime-1}\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\circ\pi

Case
\gamma=\mathtt{id}
.

In this case we have

\pi^{\prime-1}(a)=\gamma({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})=\mathtt{id}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}.

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}=\gamma(\pi^{-1}(a))=\mathtt{id}(\pi^{-1}(a))=\pi^{-1}(a).

Therefore,
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi^{\prime-1}}=(\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})=\mathtt{id}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
. Similarly,
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi^{-1}}=(\pi^{-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})=\mathtt{id}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
. Thus, the result follows by rule
(\curlywedge_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

Case
\gamma\neq\mathtt{id}
.

As observed
\gamma(\pi^{-1}(a))={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
and
\gamma({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})=\pi^{\prime-1}(a)
. Since
\mathtt{dom}(\gamma)
is finite, there is a
m\geq 3
such that
\gamma^{m}(\pi^{-1}(a))=\pi^{-1}(a)
. Then

\gamma=\underbrace{(\pi^{-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ \pi^{\prime-1}(a)\ \gamma^{4}(\pi^{-1}(a))\ \ldots\ \gamma^{m-1}(\pi^{-1}(a)))}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\circ\rho^{\prime}

where
\rho^{\prime}
is disjoint from
\gamma^{\prime}
. Then
\gamma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})_{\#})\right)
and consequently
\pi^{-1}(a),\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\in\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})_{\#})
. So it follows that
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi^{\prime-1}}=(\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
and
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi^{-1}}=(\pi^{-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
and the result follows by
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

2.

t_{0}
and
t_{1}
are abstractions.

(a)

t_{0}\equiv[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}\equiv[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
.

Then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}([a]t_{0}^{\prime},[a]t_{1}^{\prime})
implies
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash\mathtt{f^{C}}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime},(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{1}^{\prime})\approx_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}([a]t_{0}^{\prime},[a]t_{1}^{\prime})
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we have two possible branches:

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\cdot[a]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
.

Then the result follows.

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
.

Note that, by Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)) and Equivalence (Theorem
4.1
(
3
)), these two derivations are equivalent, so it’s sufficient with just one. We will work with the first one.

We claim that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
. In fact, observe that the derivation
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
is equivalent to
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}](a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
. By applying Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we derive
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash(({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{1}^{\prime}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}},a,t_{0}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime})
. By Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
), this results in
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})^{-1}\circ({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
. Since
((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})^{-1}\circ({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))=({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})
, we conclude that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
. On the other hand, because
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},a,t_{0}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime})
, Lemma
16
gives us
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}^{\prime}
. This combined with Equivalence (Theorem
4.1
(
3
)) provides us
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
. Thus, by Lemma
17
, we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
, which, by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{[a]})
, leads to
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
.

Now, using the claim we just proved and Equivalence (Theorem
4.1
(
3
)), we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
and so the result follows.

(b)

t_{0}\equiv[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}\equiv[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}
.

Then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}([a]t_{0}^{\prime},[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime})
implies
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash\mathtt{f^{C}}((a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime},(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime})\approx_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}([a]t_{0}^{\prime},[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime})
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), there are two branches to consider:

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}
.

Then the result follows.

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
.

Note that, by Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)) and Equivalence (Theorem
4.1
(
3
)), these two derivations are equivalent, so it’s sufficient to work with just one. We will work with the first one.

We claim that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
. Indeed, observe that the derivation
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}
can be rewritten as
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}](a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}
. By applying Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we derive
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash(({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}(d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{1}^{\prime}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},a,d^{\prime},t_{0}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime})
.

By Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)), we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash((d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})^{-1}\circ({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
, which is the same as
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
.

Now, by applying Lemma
16
, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}^{\prime}
. Since
((a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})^{-1}\circ(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))=({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})
, we conclude that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash((a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})^{-1}\circ(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}))\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}^{\prime}
.

By Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)), we get
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}
. This, together with the derivation
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
, and Equivalence (Theorem
4.1
(
3
))), yields
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
.

Since
(a\ d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})=(d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})
, we get
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash((d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\circ(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}))\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}^{\prime}
, which, through Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)) combined with Equivalence (Theorem
4.1
(
3
))), gives us
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}}}\vdash(d^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot t_{0}^{\prime}
. Finally, by applying rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{ab})
, we conclude that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
.

Using the claim we just proved combined with Equivalence (Theorem
4.1
(
3
)), the derivations
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}
yield

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[a]t_{0}^{\prime}\text{ and }\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{2}})\cdot[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime},

and the result follows.

(c)

For the following cases:

•

t_{0}\equiv[d^{\prime}]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}\equiv[a]t_{1}^{\prime}
.

•

t_{0}\equiv[d^{\prime}]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}\equiv[d^{\prime}]t_{1}^{\prime}

•

t_{0}\equiv[d_{1}]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}\equiv[d_{2}]t_{1}^{\prime}

•

t_{0}\equiv[d_{2}]t_{0}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}\equiv[d_{1}]t_{1}^{\prime}

The arguments for these cases are very similar to the previous ones, so we will omit them.

Lemma 20

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
iff
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t)
.

Proof

Proof by induction on structure of the term
t
. The non-trivial cases are presented below.

•

t\equiv\pi^{\prime}\cdot X
.

(\Rightarrow)

In this case, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot X
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
), it follows that
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{\pi^{\prime-1}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X})
. In particular,
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{\pi^{\prime-1}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
. Since
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{\pi^{\prime-1}}=(\pi^{\pi^{\prime-1}})_{\pi^{\prime-1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})}
and
\pi^{\prime}
does not mention names from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, we have
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{\pi^{\prime-1}}=(\pi^{\pi^{\prime-1}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
, obtaining
(\pi^{\pi^{-1}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
.

We aim to prove that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot X
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. To achieve this, it suffices to show

(\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})=(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi^{\prime-1}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X}).

–

First, let’s analyse the cases where
a
is an atom from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. Notice that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\subseteq\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})_{\#})
. Thus, for every
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{\prime}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, we have
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
, implying
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
. Since
\pi^{\prime-1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{\prime}})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{\prime}}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c^{\prime}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, this ensures that
(\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
for all
a\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
.

–

Now, suppose
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
but
a\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. Then
\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\in\mathtt{dom}((\pi^{\pi^{\prime-1}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
. From
(\pi^{\pi^{-1}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
, we get
(\pi^{\pi^{-1}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})_{\#})\right)
. Thus
\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\in\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})_{\#})
and so
(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi^{\prime-1}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X})
. Consequently, the result follows by applying rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

(\Leftarrow)

Suppose
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot X
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},\pi^{\prime})
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
), it follows that

(\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})=(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi^{\prime-1}}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}|_{X}).

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\pi^{\prime})
, we have
\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}
. Moreover,
(\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}})_{\#})\right)
, which implies
\pi^{\prime-1}(a)\in\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}})_{\#})
.

Equivalently, this means that
a\in\pi^{\prime}\cdot\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}})_{\#})
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. As a result, we have
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\pi^{\prime}\cdot\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}})_{\#})\right)
. Consequently,

\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{\pi^{\prime-1}}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}})_{\#})\right)\subseteq\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}|_{X}).

Finally, the result follows by an application of rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
.

•

t\equiv\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
.

(\Rightarrow)

In this case, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
which is the same as
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\mathtt{f^{C}}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot t_{0},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot t_{1})\approx_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
),

–

either
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot t_{0}\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot t_{1}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}
.

Then by induction, it follows that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t_{0}\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot t_{1}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t)
. By rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}})
, we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
, for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
.

–

or
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot t_{0}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot t_{0}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}
.

By Equivariance and Equivalence of Theorem
4.1
, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2}\cdot t_{0}\approx_{\tt C}t_{0}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2}\cdot t_{1}\approx_{\tt C}t_{1}
. Proceeding similarly to the analysis in the previous case, we conclude that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
.

Since
\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2})\subseteq\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})
, it remains to show that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\setminus(\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})
. Assume, by contradiction, that there exists some
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\setminus(\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}})
such that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\cdot\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})\not\approx_{\mathtt{C}}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
. Observe that, since
a\notin\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2})
, it follows that
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2}(a)=a
. Therefore,
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
can be expressed as
(a\ \pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a))\circ\rho
, where
\rho
is a disjoint from
(a\ \pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a))
. We are assuming
a\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, so we analyse
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a)
:

*

If
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a)\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, then
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
would contain the disjoint cycle
(a\ \pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a))
that does not mention any names from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, which contradicts the structure of
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
.

*

Hence,
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a)
must be an atom from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
, say
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
.

Since we proved that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}^{2})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. In particular it holds for all
a\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. Then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}(a)\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
, which is the same as
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})^{\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
. By Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)), this implies
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\cdot\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
and so the result follows by Equivalence of Theorem
4.1
.

(\Leftarrow)

Suppose
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}\notin\mathtt{Atm}(\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}},t)
. By Lemma
19
, we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t_{0}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}}}\vdash(a\ {\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}c_{1}})\curlywedge_{\tt C}t_{1}
for all
a\in\mathtt{dom}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}})\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
. By induction, we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t_{0}
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t_{1}
and thus by rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{\mathtt{f^{C}}})
we get
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}\mathtt{f^{C}}(t_{0},t_{1})
.

Lemma 21

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
iff
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
.

Proof

The left-to-right case follows by Lemma
15
(
1
). For the right-to-left case, assume that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. By Soundness (Theorem
4.2
(
1
)), we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. As a consequence of Pitts’ equivalence (Lemma
2
), we have
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vDash\pi_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. Therefore, the result follows by Completeness (Theorem
4.2
(
2
)).

Lemma 22

Let
I
be a non-empty, finite set of indices. Suppose, for each
i\in I
, that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{i}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. Let
\pi
be a permutation and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}
such that

\pi\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathtt{dom}((\pi_{i})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}})\right)\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle\{(\pi_{i})_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\mid i\in I\}\right\rangle.

(7)

Then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
.

Proof

By Lemma
21
, we have:
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash(\pi_{i})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
and
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash(\pi_{i})_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
, for all
i\in I
. Write
\pi
as
\pi=\eta_{1}\circ\eta_{2}
where
\eta_{1}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left(\left(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathtt{dom}((\pi_{i})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}})\right)\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\right)
and
\eta_{2}\in\left\langle\{(\pi_{i})_{\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\mid i\in I\}\right\rangle
. Thus, on one hand the former combined with Lemmas
20
and
18
yields
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\eta_{1}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. On the other hand, the latter combined with Lemma
1
yields
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\eta_{2}\curlywedge_{\tt C}t
. Then the result follows by Lemma
1
(
1
).

Given contexts
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
and
\Upsilon^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}
and
\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
a

\mathsf{N}

-substitution. We introduce some notations:

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\Upsilon^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
for
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\Upsilon^{\prime}\sigma
.

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash s\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
for
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash s\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma
.

•

\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}t\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
for
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}t\sigma
.

Lemma 23
(Preservation by substitution)

Suppose
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
and
\Upsilon^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}
are contexts and let
\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
be a substitution such that
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\Upsilon^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
. If
\Upsilon^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash s\approx_{\tt C}t
then
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash s\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
.

Proof

Proof by induction on the last rule applied. The only non-trivial case is the rule
({{\approx}}_{\tt C}\,\mathbf{var})
. In this case,
s\equiv\pi_{1}\cdot X
and
t\equiv\pi_{2}\cdot X
. Hence,
\Upsilon^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X
. By Inversion (Theorem
4.1
(
1
)), we have
\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathtt{P}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$$}}(\Upsilon^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})={\mathcal{G}}\left(\mathtt{Atm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\#})\right){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\circ}\left\langle\mathtt{perm}((\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}|_{X})_{\curlywedge_{\tt C}})\right\rangle
. By hypothesis,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\sigma
for every
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}Y\in\Upsilon^{\prime}
. In particular,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\sigma
for every
\pi\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\in\Upsilon^{\prime}|_{X}
. By Lemma
22
, we obtain
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1})\curlywedge_{\tt C}X\sigma
, which is the same as
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(\pi_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1})\cdot X\sigma\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma
. By Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)), this is equivalent to
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\pi_{1}\cdot X\sigma\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X\sigma
Therefore,
\Upsilon_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash\pi\cdot X\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\approx_{\tt C}\pi_{2}\cdot X\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
.

Appendix 0.E
Proofs of Section
5
- Nominal
\mathtt{C}
-Unification

For the rest of this section, for a solvable problem
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}=\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr
and a solution
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
, we will write
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(Pr)\sigma
to abbreviate that
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash s\sigma\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma
for all
s\approx_{\tt C}^{?}t\in Pr
.

Lemma 24

The relation
\lesssim
defines a quasiorder (i.e. reflexive and transitive) in
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
.

Proof

•

Reflexivity.
Just note that for every pair
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
, we have
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\lesssim_{\mathtt{id}}\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}
.

•

Transitivity.
Suppose
\left\langle\Psi_{1},\sigma_{1}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}},\left\langle\Psi_{2},\sigma_{2}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}},\left\langle\Psi_{3},\sigma_{3}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
are such that
\left\langle\Psi_{1},\sigma_{1}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}}\lesssim_{\delta_{1}}\left\langle\Psi_{2},\sigma_{2}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}
and
\left\langle\Psi_{2},\sigma_{2}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\lesssim_{\delta_{2}}\left\langle\Psi_{3},\sigma_{3}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}
. Then, by definition, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}
and

1.

(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash\Psi_{1}\delta_{1}
and
(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}_{2}}}\vdash X\sigma_{2}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{1}\delta_{1}
for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
.

2.

(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash\Psi_{2}\delta_{2}
and
(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash X\sigma_{3}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{2}\delta_{2}
for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
.

Take
\delta=\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
. We know
(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash\Psi_{2}\delta_{2}
. Moreover, we have
(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash\Psi_{1}\delta_{1}
. Applying Lemma
23
yields
(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash\Psi_{1}\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}
.

For all
X\in\mathbb{V}
, we have that
(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash X\sigma_{2}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{1}\delta_{1}
. By
(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash\Psi_{2}\delta_{2}
, Lemma
23
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}
, we obtain
(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash X\sigma_{2}\delta_{2}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{1}\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
. Moreover, we know that
(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash X\sigma_{3}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{2}\delta_{2}
for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
. Therefore, by transitivity of
\approx_{\tt C}
, it follows that
(\Psi_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{3}}}\vdash X\sigma_{3}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{1}\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
.

Lemma 25
(Closure by Instantiation)

Suppose
\left\langle\Psi_{1},\sigma_{1}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
and
\left\langle\Psi_{1},\sigma_{1}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Psi_{2},\sigma_{2}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}
. Then
\left\langle\Psi_{2},\sigma_{2}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
.

Proof

Suppose
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}=\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr
. Let
\left\langle\Psi_{1},\sigma_{1}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
. Then by definition, we have
(\Psi_{1})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}}\vdash(Pr)\sigma_{1}
and
(\Psi_{1})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}_{1}}}\vdash X\sigma_{1}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{1}\sigma_{1}
for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
. From
\left\langle\Psi_{1},\sigma_{1}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Psi_{2},\sigma_{2}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}
, we know that there exist a substitution
\delta
such that:

1.

(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash\Psi_{1}\delta
.

2.

(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash X\sigma_{2}\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma_{1}\delta
, for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
;

Claim
(1)

We claim that
(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash t\sigma_{2}\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma_{1}\delta
for all nominal term
t
. The proof proceeds by straightforward induction on the structure of the nominal term
t
utilizing item 2 as a key component of the argument.

Claim
(2)

We claim that
(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash(Pr)\sigma_{2}
. For each
s\approx_{\tt C}^{?}t\in Pr
, we have
(\Psi_{1})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{1}}}\vdash s\sigma_{1}\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma_{1}
. By Lemma
23
, it follows that
(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash s\sigma_{1}\delta\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma_{1}\delta
. By Claim 1 and transitivity of
\approx_{\tt C}
, it follows that
(\Psi_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}_{2}}}\vdash s\sigma_{2}\approx_{\tt C}t\sigma_{2}
.

For the termination proof, we define the
size of a nominal term

t
, denoted by
|t|
, inductively by:
|a|:=1,|\pi\cdot X|:=1,|\mathtt{f}(\tilde{t})_{n}|:=1+|t_{1}|+\ldots+|t_{n}|
for
\mathtt{f}\in\Sigma
, and
|[a]t|:=1+|t|
.
The
size of a constraint
is defined by
|s\approx_{\tt C}^{?}t|:=|s|+|t|
. As a consequence, for all term
t
and permutation
\pi
, we have that
|\pi\cdot t|=|t|
.

See
5.1

Proof

Define a measure for the size of a problem
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}=\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr
as
[\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}]=(n,M)
, where
n
is the number of distinct variables occurring in the constraints of
Pr
and
M
is the multiset of the sizes of the equality constraints that are not of the form
\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}^{?}X
that are occurring in
Pr
. To compare
[\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}]
and
[\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}]
we use the lexicographic order
>_{lex}
that is defined upon the product of the usual order on natural numbers,
>
, and its multiset extension
>_{mul}
, that is,
>_{lex}\;\triangleq\;>\times>_{mul}
.

Lemma 26

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}
be a problem such that
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{*}{\Longrightarrow}{\cal M}
without using instantiating rules. Assume
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{*}{\Longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}\in{\cal M}
. Then

1.

\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})=\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime})
.

2.

If
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
contains inconsistent constraints, then
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})=\emptyset
.

Proof

For the first item the proof follows by induction on the length of the derivation
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{*}{\Longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
.

(Base).

The base of the induction corresponds to the case where
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{0}{\Longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
, which implies that
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}=\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
and hence
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})=\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime})
.

(Inductive step).

Now, we are going to prove that the result holds for reductions of length
n>0
, that is,
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{n}{\Longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
. Our induction hypothesis is

If
{\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}}_{1}\overset{n-1}{\Longrightarrow}{\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}}_{2}
, then
\mathcal{U}({\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}}_{1})=\mathcal{U}({\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}}_{2})

(I.H.)

Note that we can rewrite
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{n}{\Longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
as
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{n-1}{\Longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\Longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
. By (
I.H.
), it follows that
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})=\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime})
. It remains to analyse the last step of the reduction, specifically
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\Longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
. We present a illustrative case: The last rule is
(var)
. In this case,
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}=\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr_{0}\uplus\{\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}^{?}\pi^{\prime}\cdot X\}\Longrightarrow\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr_{0}\cup\{\pi^{\prime-1}\circ\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}^{?}X\}=\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime}
, where
\pi^{\prime}\neq\mathtt{id}
. If
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime})
, then

\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(\pi\cdot X)\sigma\approx_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot X\sigma
. Since
(\rho\cdot X)\sigma\equiv\rho\cdot X\sigma
, it follows that
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash\pi\cdot(X\sigma)\approx_{\tt C}\pi^{\prime}\cdot(X\sigma)
. By Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)), we have
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(\pi^{\prime-1}\circ\pi)\cdot(X\sigma)\approx_{\tt C}X\sigma
. Therefore,
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})
and hence
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime})
. For the other inclusion the argument is the same.

The second item of the lemma follows directly from the fact that inconsistent constraints are not derivable, therefore have no solutions.

See
5.2

Proof

By induction on the number of steps in the reduction
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{*}{\Longrightarrow}\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{nf}}
.

(Base).

This corresponds to the case where
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{0}{\Longrightarrow}\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{nf}}
. In this case,
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{nf}}=\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}=\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr
and so
Pr
contains only consistent constraints. Then
Pr=\{\pi_{i}\cdot X_{i}\approx_{\tt C}^{?}X_{i}\mid i=1,\ldots,n\}
. By construction, the pair
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}=\{\left\langle\Psi,Id\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\}
, where
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}=\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.\{\pi_{i}\curlywedge_{\tt C}X_{i}\mid i=1,\ldots,n\}
, satisfies
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\vdash\pi_{i}\cdot X_{i}Id\approx_{\tt C}X_{i}Id
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
. Therefore,
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
.

For the second item, for any other
\left\langle\Phi,\tau\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
, by definition,
\tau
is such that
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(Pr)\tau
which is the same as
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\Psi\tau
. Moreover,
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash X\tau\approx_{\tt C}XId\tau
follows for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
by reflexivity. Therefore, this proves that
\left\langle\Psi,Id\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Phi,\tau\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
and the second item follows.

(Inductive step).

Now, we are going to prove that the result holds for reductions of length
n>0
, that is,
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{n}{\Longrightarrow}\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{nf}}
. We rewrite it as follows:
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\Longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\overset{n-1}{\Longrightarrow}\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{nf}}
.

•

Suppose
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\Longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}
by some non-instantiating simplification. Then using Lemma
26
, we know that
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})=\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime})
.

By induction, we have
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime})
. Consequently,
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
. Since no instantiation rule was used, by the construction of
\left\langle-\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
we have that
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
and the first item follows.

For the second item, for any other
\left\langle\Phi,\tau\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
, by induction, there is some
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
such that
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Phi,\tau\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
and the result follows because
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
.

•

Suppose
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\overset{\theta}{\Longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}
by an instantiating rule, say
(inst_{1})
. So
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}=\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr\uplus\{\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}^{?}t\}\overset{\theta}{\Longrightarrow}\reflectbox{$\mathsf{N}$}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}}.Pr\theta=\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}
where
\theta=[X\mapsto\pi^{-1}\cdot t]
and
X\notin\mathtt{Var}(t)
.

Let
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
, so by construction
\sigma=\theta\sigma^{\prime}
and
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma^{\prime}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
. By induction,
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime})
which implies
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(Pr\theta)\sigma^{\prime}
, that is the same as
\Psi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\vdash(Pr)(\theta\sigma^{\prime})
and thus
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
which proves the first item.

It remains to prove that
\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
is a complete set of solutions of
\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}
. So, take
\left\langle\Phi,\tau\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C})
. By definition,
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(Pr\uplus\{\pi\cdot X\approx_{\tt C}^{?}t\})\tau
. In particular,
\Phi^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(\pi\cdot X)\tau\approx_{\tt C}t\tau
which, by Equivariance (Theorem
4.1
(
2
)) and the fact that permutations and substitutions commute, results in
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash X\tau\approx_{\tt C}(\pi^{-1}\cdot t)\tau
that is the same as
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash X\tau\approx_{\tt C}X\theta\tau
.

Let
\tau^{\prime}
be the substitution that acts just like
\tau
, only
X\tau\equiv X
(remember that
X\tau\not\equiv X
). Then since
X\notin\mathtt{Var}(X\theta)
, we have
X\theta\tau\equiv X\theta\tau^{\prime}
and hence
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash X\tau\approx_{\tt C}X\theta\tau^{\prime}
. For all other variable
Y
, we have
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash Y\tau\approx_{\tt C}Y\tau^{\prime}
. Since
Y\theta\equiv Y
, we conclude that
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash Z\tau\approx_{\tt C}Z\theta\tau^{\prime}
holds for all variable
Z\in\mathbb{V}
. Then
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash s\tau\approx_{\tt C}s\theta\tau^{\prime}
for all nominal term
s
. As a consequence, because
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(Pr)\tau
we conclude
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(Pr)(\theta\tau^{\prime})
. This is the same as
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash(Pr\theta)\tau^{\prime}
. So
\left\langle\Phi,\tau^{\prime}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime})
and by inductive hypothesis there is some
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}^{\prime\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
such that
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Phi,\tau^{\prime}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
. By construction,
\left\langle\Psi,\theta\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\in\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{\tt C}\right\rangle_{\mathtt{sol}}
.

Claim
(1)

We claim that
\left\langle\Psi,\theta\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Phi,\theta\tau^{\prime}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
. Since
\left\langle\Psi,\sigma\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Phi,\tau^{\prime}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
we know that there is some
\delta
such that
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\Psi\delta
and
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash Z\tau^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}Z\sigma\delta
for all
Z\in\mathbb{V}
. Then,
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash u\tau^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}u\sigma\delta
holds for all terms
u
, in particular, for every
u\equiv X\theta
where
X\in V
. So
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash X\theta\tau^{\prime}\approx_{\tt C}X\theta\sigma\delta
for all
X\in\mathbb{V}
, proving the claim.

Claim
(2)

We claim that
\left\langle\Phi,\theta\tau^{\prime}\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\lesssim\left\langle\Phi,\tau\right\rangle_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}
. In fact, take
\delta=Id
. Then
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash\Phi Id
trivially. Furthermore, we proved that
\Phi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\vec{c}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\vdash Z\tau\approx_{\tt C}^{?}Z\theta\tau^{\prime}Id
for all variable
Z\in\mathbb{V}
.

The result follows by combining Claims (1) and (2) and using Lemma
24
, concluding the proof of Correctness.
